### Page 1

Paris, 23 October 2026

EcoFlash

Q3 Growth in advanced economies: solid despite shocks

Growth looks to have held up in Q3 despite successive shocks (see our analysis), as it did in 2025 and H1 2026. A longer 
Middle East conflict has fed into more persistent inflation; drought has worsened in many countries, and interest rates 
have risen further – at the short end, with both the ECB and the Fed raising policy rates in September, and even more at 
the long end. However, our nowcasts show that growth is bending without breaking in most advanced economies. GDP 
figures (due on 29 October in the US and 30 October for the euro area and its member states) are expected to confirm 
that. In the Euro area, momentum looks stable (excluding Ireland, whose GDP is volatile). In Germany, growth has been 
uninterrupted since Q4 2025, while France is likely to have returned to growth after stagnating in H1. In the US, growth 
is likely to have gathered pace, according to the GDP Now, still driven by AI, but increasingly by other sectors as well.

Euro area: resilience beneath the surface. Our nowcast points to growth slowing to 0.3% in q/q, though the pace is stable 
excluding Ireland (Irish GDP is volatile and had markedly boosted the Q2 figure). Excluding Ireland, growth should hold at its H2 
average, a sign of the bloc’s underlying resilience. Also, momentum has broadened to a growing number of sectors ( see our 
analysis). While industrial production stalled over the summer, services remained buoyant. The services PMI rebounded sharply 
in Q3 and sits firmly in expansion territory (53.0 in September). Improving job-related survey data (PMI and European Commission 
surveys) and a stable unemployment rate (6.4% in August) hint at more hiring in Q3 – a support for household consumption that 
partly offsets purchasing-power losses from persistent inflation. Corporate investment is underpinned by buoyant new investment 
lending (8.7% y/y In August, 12-month cumulative). 
France: growing, nevertheless. The succession of shocks does not appear to have prevented France from returning to 
growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q, according to our nowcast. One-off factors accounted for the stall in activity in H1. In Q3, the drought 
worsened and industrial production remained subdued. However, market services production rebounded in July and August, 
notably in business services, restaurants, digital and real estate. The latter points to residential investment recovering after a weak 
H1. Corporate investment likely followed, as reflected in buoyant credit flows and AI-related investment. Household consumption 
is likely to have improved slightly on higher electric vehicle sales.  
Euro area  France

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas  Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas

Germany: growth still solid. The H1 rebound in activity is moderating but continues. Our nowcast points to a fourth straight 
quarter of growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q (after 0.3% in Q4 2025, 0.4% in Q1 2026, and 0.3% in Q2). Manufacturing activity eased 
slightly, according to the September PMI survey (53.9, down 0.4 m/m). Government spending will remain growth-supportive, with 
defence and infrastructure outlays gaining traction. Infrastructure spending continues to underpin activity in public works, which 
stands at its highest since November 2021 (2.5% 3m/3m in August). Exports (4.4% y/y between January and August) remain the 
main growth engine, helped by a pivot towards Europe (7.2%). 
United States: an expansion undeterred by inflation. Growth is likely to have gathered pace in Q3. The Atlanta Fed’s GDP 
Now points to a 3.5% annualized growth rate (AR), close to our own 3.7% forecast (0.9% non -AR) and up from 2.2% in 
Q2. Domestic demand remains the engine. Consumption is weathering the pickup in inflation, while the AI-driven capex cycle 
remains robust – as reflected in retail sales and nondefence capital-goods orders (excl. aircraft). As a result, growth in final sales

### Page 2

to private domestic purchasers 1 should print above 4% AR again. By contrast, external trade should remain a drag on growth, 
reflecting AI-related import needs. Survey data point to mome ntum that is both solid and broad -based. The ISM indices have 
remained firmly in growth territory (54.9 quarterly average for manufacturing and services alike). At the same time, they highlight 
persistent price and supply-chain pressures.

Germany   United States

Rising fuel prices and the war with Iran have signiﬁcantly
increased the chances of a Democrat-controlled Congress. This
could mean greater pricing pressure on pharma and more
healthcare aﬀordability measures. However, surprises do
happen. We therefore assess three possible scenarios and what
they mean for healthcare and pharma
Diederik Stadig
Republicans and Democrats want more domestic manufacturing and lower
drug prices, but there are diﬀerences in how they get there

### Page 2

The US midterm elections take place on 3 November. All 435 seats
in the House of Representatives and 35 of the 100 Senate seats are
up for election. With Trump’s approval rating hitting a record low
according to a Fox News poll, it is no wonder that the pollsters
currently suggest the Democrats lead by around 8 percentage
points nationally. A Democratic takeover of Congress therefore
seems most likely, but a Democratic clean sweep is not certain.

We look at what the possible outcomes could mean for healthcare
and pharma.
The key differences
For healthcare, US voters face a clear choice: Democrats want to
expand Medicaid and insurance coverage, as well as permanently
extend Aﬀordable Care Act (ACA) subsidies. Republicans favour the
opposite approach, with greater responsibility shifted to individual
states.
For pharma, on the other hand, the old political divide of
"Democrats regulate, Republicans leave industry alone" is largely

gone. Both parties now see the pharma and biotech sector as key
to national security, and therefore want more domestic
manufacturing. They also both want lower drug prices. The
diﬀerence is how they get there. Democrats lean toward
government negotiation under Medicare and incentivising domestic
manufacturing through other means than tariﬀs; Republicans lean
toward most-favoured-nation (MFN) pricing and trade pressure. In
short, the policy risks diﬀer slightly depending on the outcome, but

the objectives are largely bipartisan.
Midterm election scenarios and their impact
on US consumers and Europe as well as
healthcare and pharma

Coverage retrenchment:Medicaid restrictions and
spending restraint continue, while restoration of
enhanced ACA subsidies remains unlikely. More
responsibility shifts towards states, private
insurers and consumers. Patients and providers in
Medicaid-heavy markets face greater exposure to
coverage losses and uncompensated care.
MFN, tariffs and onshoring:Trump gains more
room to consolidate MFN pricing, tariff exemptions
and domestic -production commitments.

Continuation of current situation: Europe faces
strong price pressure, while the US pulls in
investment, pricing and supply -chain pressure
remain manageable for the industry .
Scrutiny without reform:Democrats can
investigate Medicaid cuts, ACA affordability and
administration policy, and pass House bills to
restore coverage. A Republican Senate and
presidential veto would block most substantial
changes, leaving the current coverage framework
largely intact . Change for patients is minimal .

More confrontation, limited change:House
committees investigate MFN agreements, tariffs,
FDA decisions and drug pricing. Executive trade
and pricing policies largely continue, but hearings,
document requests and political uncertainty
increase. Bipartisan action possible on PBMs and
hospitals. Opportunities for Europe to slow -walk
price negotiations increase.
Push to restore and expand coverage:Democrats
seek to reverse or soften Medicaid cuts, restore

enhanced ACA subsidies and strengthen access to
healthcare , which would lower premiums and out -

leverage over spending and legislation increases,
although Trump’s veto and Senate procedural

MFN deals. Desire to onshore production remains ,

Scenario 1: Republican revival
A Republican victory will embolden President Trump to entrench his

healthcare and industrial policy legacy during his ﬁnal two years in
oﬃce. His administration would face fewer congressional obstacles
to implementing Medicaid restrictions, maintaining a more limited
federal role in insurance coverage and resisting restoration of
enhanced ACA subsidies.
For consumers as well as healthcare providers and insurers, the
main risk would be further coverage erosion. Hospitals in Medicaid-
heavy markets could see more uncompensated care.

Pharmaceutical volumes could be aﬀected where patients lose
coverage or struggle to aﬀord prescriptions.
For pharma, Republican control of Congress would give Trump
greater scope to embed MFN pricing and use tariﬀ exemptions and
company agreements to further secure US manufacturing
commitments. Pharmaceutical companies would gain greater
clarity about the direction of policy, but not relief from pricing or
supply-chain pressure. In short, this would be the scenario with the

greatest continuity in pharma policy.
Scenario 2: Trump constrained
A Democratic House could obstruct legislation, control committees
and launch investigations. But bills restoring ACA subsidies or

### Page 4

mitigating Medicaid cuts would face a Republican Senate and,
ultimately, Trump’s veto. Healthcare policy would therefore become
more confrontational but not necessarily very diﬀerent in practice.
Coverage losses and stricter Medicaid eligibility rules would remain
immediate issues, while Democrats would use hearings and must-
pass spending legislation to put pressure on the administration.
Pharma would face detailed scrutiny of MFN deals, tariﬀs, and the

implementation of Medicare negotiations. Yet neither party would
control all the levers required for sweeping reform. This could create
room for narrower bipartisan measures, particularly around
Pharmacy Beneﬁt Managers (PBM) and hospitals. Broader reform
would probably stall, meaning that not much would change for the
US consumer.
Scenario 3: Democratic clean sweep
Control of both chambers would give Democrats their strongest
platform to restore ACA subsidies, soften Medicaid cuts and make

healthcare aﬀordability a central condition in budget and defence
negotiations. This would lower premiums and out-of-pocket costs
for US consumers, while helping more people retain or gain health
insurance. Coverage expansion is also likely to improve hospital
ﬁnances and prescription volumes.
For pharma, Democrats would likely slow MFN momentum by
demanding transparency around the MFN deals as well as
challenging their design and cost. It is also likely that they would

want to prioritise expanding or accelerating the Medicare drug-price
negotiations agreed under the IRA, alongside greater scrutiny of
launch prices and PBMs.
Moreover, stronger congressional protection of National Institutes of
Health (NIH) funding, scientiﬁc institutions and Food and Drug
Administration (FDA) review capacity would support biotech and
early-stage innovation. If Democrats truly make drug-price
negotiations a priority, then this scenario could lead to a mixed

outcome: greater price pressure, but a stronger public research base
and a better-funded FDA.

### Page 5

Why a Democratic clean sweep would be
good news for European governments
For Europe, pressure on the pharma industry to localise in the US
will remain under all three scenarios. Democrats may criticise the
means, but they also view pharmaceutical supply chains as a
national-security issue. Republican and Democratic policy
objectives therefore broadly align, while Trump retains substantial
executive authority over trade.
The biggest diﬀerence is that a Democratic clean sweep would

demand greater transparency around MFN deals and increase price
pressure through Medicare negotiations. That scrutiny could raise
the chances of European governments slow-walking their own price
negotiations in the expectation that MFN terms may be delayed or
revised. By contrast, a Republican clean sweep would reinforce
policy continuity and further strengthen the pull for manufacturing
investment to move to the US.
Europe’s structural competitiveness issue therefore remains under

all scenarios, but is most pronounced if the Republicans win both
the House and the Senate. Unless Europe improves
commercialisation, capital market depth and harmonises
regulation, it risks losing both production to the US and intellectual
property/partnerships to China.
Beijing is now more likely to block licensing
deals than Washington
China will also keep facing bipartisan pressure. Both parties now
view pharma and biotech as key to national security interests and

support greater domestic manufacturing and reduced foreign
dependencies. However, China’s dominance in active
pharmaceutical ingredients is unlikely to wane any time soon.
The US imports more than half of its
pharmaceuticals from China

Furthermore, commercial demand for Chinese innovation will not

disappear simply because supply-chain policy becomes more
restrictive. We still forecast Chinese outlicensing to increase to more
than $250bn this year, nearly double the deal value of 2025
despite geopolitical tensions. What's more, licensing restrictions are
now more likely to come from Beijing than Washington. The sector
has convinced US policymakers that these deals are needed to
access innovation, while China is considering restrictions on
acquisitions of biopharma platforms involving key technologies.

These deals will continue under any scenario, but especially if
Republicans retain control of Congress. If there is a Democratic
clean sweep, however, Trump will tend to focus more on foreign and
trade policy, meaning China could end up in the crosshairs. This, in
turn, could mean greater scrutiny of licensing and slower growth –
not a decline – in dealmaking in 2027.

Document 487: Why hasn’t there been a major financial crisis in recent years_ (2026-11-03)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Why hasn’t there been a major financial crisis in recent years_ (2026-11-03).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 19
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Why hasn’t there been a major 
financial crisis in recent years?

1 
Authors 
Henry Allen 
Macro Strategist

Peter Sidorov, CFA 
Senior Economist

Camilla Siazon 
Research Analyst

November 3, 2026 
— The economist Hyman Minsky warned, in essence, that “stability is destabilising” . He 
argued that a lengthy period of financial stability could be problematic, as it induced 
risk-taking and complacency amongst investors, laying the foundation for the next 
crisis. For example, lenders relax their terms and borrowers are more willing to extend 
themselves, as nobody wants to miss out. This dynamic makes economies inherently 
prone to financial panics and boom-bust cycles. 
— But since the 2008 crisis, and the subsequent sovereign crisis in the Eurozone, we 
have yet to see another financial crisis happen on a scale that had a lasting market 
and macroeconomic impact. There have been clear moments of concern, as in early 
2023 with the regional bank turmoil, but nothing that has caused a lasting impact. So 
this led us to ask why. 
— In this paper, we examine why the post-GFC era has seen fewer financial crises. 
Explanations include the reforms made after 2008 to avoid a repeat, the lower 
proportion of financial assets held by the banking system, as well as the speedier 
reaction of policymakers when wobbles have occurred. We then consider a few 
candidates that could cause the next crisis, including AI vulnerabilities, private credit, 
and stretched fiscal positions. 
— Ultimately, financial crises haven’t been abolished, but the reforms of recent years 
have made it harder for shocks to propagate through the traditional banking system. 
Moreover, policymakers have become far more inclined to intervene when initial signs 
of stress have emerged.

### Page 2

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
Section 1: What is a financial crisis, and what causes them?
There isn’t a single technical definition of a “financial crisis”, and they come in many
different types. Commonly, we think of banking crises like 2008, but they can also
encompass currency/balance of payments crises, debt crises and other forms too.
Given currency and sovereign crises have been more common in emerging markets
(the UK's ERM crisis in 1992 and the euro crisis being the two most notable DM

occurrences), historical analysis in developed economies has tended to focus
heavily on banking crises. Non-bank funding or market-liquidity crises are often not
categorised separately in the literature, but they have become more common in
recent years. This includes the "dash-for-cash" stress at the onset of the Covid
pandemic in March 2020 and the 2022 LDI crisis in the UK.
But for all the different types of financial crisis, there are common themes between

them. Often, they are accompanied by asset bubbles that burst in a non-linear
fashion. We generally see a severe tightening in credit conditions, which in turn
flows through into a broader economic contraction. Frequently, there are
contagion-like dynamics that can be difficult to stop. And in the modern world, they
usually require policymakers to step in, stabilising the situation with interventions.
At risk of oversimplification, we note four factors that determine the emergence and

the severity of a financial crisis. The first three are well-established in the financial
literature, sometimes pooled together into a build-up and a crisis phase
(Brunnermeier & Oehmke 2013), while the fourth has become increasingly
important over the past 15-20 years:
1.  Underlying imbalances. Bubbles and financial imbalances build during
the run-up phase, leaving accumulated risks that materialise when the
crisis erupts. This is the build-up typically described by the early phases of

Kindleberger's model of financial crises.
2.  Trigger. This is the shock that turns an existing vulnerability into clear
financial stress. These are often very difficult to predict and we mostly leave
these outside the focus of this report.
3.  Amplification and financial system fragility. Various factors determine
whether a shock propagates through the financial system, and the
intensity of that shock. These include leverage, liquidity and maturity

mismatches, interconnectedness, and the concentration of exposures.
Other channels in the financial system can then amplify the shock,
including bank runs, fire sales and margin calls, and negative net worth
effects, sovereign–bank loops etc.
4.  Intervention/policy response. This has been increasingly important in
recent decades as the size and scope of policy response to crises has
increased. The outright size can vary, and intervention can occur across

several dimensions, ranging from direct intervention in financial markets,
to counter-cyclical macroeconomic policy that mitigates the impact on the
real economy.
Clearly, these factors are not independent and the interaction between them is what
determines the severity of a financial crisis.

### Page 3

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 3
One important aspect is the joint role that underlying imbalances and amplification
play. While theoretical models such as Diamond & Dybvig highlight the potential for
a bank run that triggers a broad crisis, empirical research shows that in practice, it
is exceedingly rare for a bank run to lead to bank failure in the absence of an
underlying solvency issue (Correia, Luck and Verner 2026). In other words, the

presence of amplification channels alone does not lead to a crisis.
Conversely, in the absence of amplification, it is possible to get significant
turbulence in asset prices, but without a financial crisis. For instance, a bear market,
wider credit spreads, or even a recession do not in and of themselves imply there
is a financial crisis. This was seen clearly with the bursting of the dot com bubble,
a severe equity decline but not a financial crisis, though it did cause a recession. At

the same time, in the chart below, we can see several US equity bear markets that
took place outside of recessions. On other occasions, however, a “Minsky
Moment” can then happen, where even a seemingly minor event can trigger a high-
profile incident that cascades across the financial system.
Figure 1: S&P 500 drawdowns over time, with shaded recessions showing select episodes when the index fell over 20%
without coinciding with a recession
Source :Bloomberg Finance LP, NBER, Deutsche Bank Research

Looking back through history, we can see how variation in these factors has led to
different regimes over time in terms of frequency/severity of financial factors.

### Page 4

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
Section 2. Why have financial crises been more or less likely
in certain periods of history?
1. Pre-WWII: More frequent financial instability, albeit under a very
different system.
Financial instability was a recurrent feature of the pre-WWII era. During the 19th
and early 20th centuries, there were frequent panics that often led to major slumps.
For instance, the Panic of 1873 sparked a period often known as the “Long

Depression”, which extended into the 1890s for many countries.
In part, financial panics occurred more frequently because policy operated under
very different settings. For instance, the Gold Standard as the monetary system
constrained a countercyclical policy response and transmitted deflation
internationally, while falling prices increased real debt burdens. Moreover, the
Federal Reserve wasn’t established until 1913, and the lack of an effective lender of

last resort meant there was a private intervention during the US Panic of 1907. The
first wave of globalisation also brought new vulnerabilities. Between 1870 and
1913, many emerging economies accumulated debts denominated in gold or
foreign currency, leaving them exposed when capital inflows reversed or exchange
rates weakened.
The biggest and most famous crisis occurred in 1929, when the Wall Street Crash
of that year sparked the Great Depression. The length and depth of that crisis helped

to stimulate the study of modern macroeconomics, with huge efforts to prevent a
repeat over the years ahead. Indeed, the failures of the response to the Great
Depression played a large role in the much swifter response to the 2008 financial
crisis, helping to avoid a second Great Depression.
That experience established much of the modern financial safety net that exists to
this day. For instance in the US, the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation was

established in 1933. The US and many other countries came off the Gold Standard
as well in this period. There were also regulatory reforms, and the Glass-Steagall Act
separated out commercial from investment banking.
2. 1944-1971: The Bretton Woods era coincided with fewer financial
crises
Around the end of WWII, economic policymakers sought to create an international
monetary system for the post-war era. The relative financial calm that followed

reflected a combination of restrictions on international capital movement, tightly
regulated domestic financial systems, and a system of fixed exchange rates that
aimed to provide stability and prevent the competitive devaluations that occurred
in the 1930s. This saw exchange rates pegged against the US Dollar, which in turn
was convertible to gold at $35 per ounce.
At the same time, capital controls helped preserve monetary policy flexibility, while

maintaining the system of fixed exchange rates. These controls had several
downsides but they also insulated countries from “sudden stop” crises that can
occur, when capital rapidly flees a country. Moreover, capital controls prevented
the rapid buildup of cross-border systemic leverage. So it was more difficult to
generate a global financial crisis in the same way.

### Page 5

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
Figure 2: Gold price (USD/oz, log scale)
10
100

1920 1940 1960 1980 2000 2020
In the Bretton Woods
era, the dollar was tied
to gold
Source : Finaeon, Deutsche Bank
Alongside capital controls, there were also domestic reforms in the aftermath of the
Great Depression, which sought to avoid a repeat. As mentioned earlier, the US
Glass-Steagall Act of 1933 separated commercial banking from investment

banking. Another example of the era’s regulated financial system in the US was
Regulation Q, which imposed maximum interest rates that commercial banks
could pay on deposits. That meant depositors could not search for yield and cause
a run on deposits as easily. Another rationale was that with institutions not
competing for deposits, it would avoid a compression in the margin between the
rates banks paid on their lending and borrowing, which would disincentivise a push

into riskier investments.
Finally, economic growth was also stronger in this period, with the period between
WWII and the 1973 oil shock often described as the “Golden Age”. This played a role
in avoiding financial crises too, as faster growth helped keep debt default rates
down, avoiding the kind of “trigger” events above that might spark a financial crisis.
Coupled with financial repression, strong growth also helped to bring down the
high post-war public debt burdens.

Over time however, the peg between the dollar and gold found itself under growing
strain. The economist Robert Triffin identified the “Triffin dilemma”. Supplying the
world with dollar reserves resulted in outstanding dollar liabilities around the world
that exceeded the gold reserves in the US, weakening confidence that dollars could
ultimately be redeemed for gold. Conversely, restricting dollar supply to protect
those reserves and preserve convertibility risked depriving the system of the

liquidity needed to support trade and growth.
These tensions intensified in the late 1960s amid expansionary US policies,
including spending on the Vietnam War and Great Society programmes. The
continued drain in US gold reserves eventually led the US to suspend the
convertibility of the US Dollar into gold, effectively ending the Bretton Woods
system. Initially, there were attempts to bring some kind of fixed-exchange rate
system back, but the world of fiat currencies (with no link to a precious metal)

persists to this day.

### Page 6

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
3. 1971-2008: Financial crises become more frequent after the
breakdown of Bretton Woods to the Global Financial Crisis
After the breakdown of the Bretton Woods system, financial crises became a more
regular occurrence. While the end of the gold convertibility did not by itself trigger
future crises, it opened the door for wider financial liberalisation, with the resulting
rise in capital mobility and looser domestic financial regulation then helping to

facilitate a global build-up of imbalances.
The 1980s and 1990s were a period of frequent crisis events. The transition away
from capital controls meant that capital could flow more freely across borders.
However, this could also cause a “sudden stop” if investors suddenly pulled their
capital out of a country, contributing to bank runs and currency collapses.
Moreover, many emerging markets borrowed in foreign currencies like the US
Dollar, meaning that a depreciation in their own currency would see this USD-

denominated debt surge in local currency terms. This meant it was a particular
problem if US interest rates were increasing, as it became harder for many
emerging markets to repay their debts. This was a contributing factor behind the
Latin American debt crisis in the early 1980s. And the rate hikes also had domestic
ramifications, being a contributing factor behind the Savings and Loan crisis in the
United States.
With capital flowing more freely, that also played a role in the 1994 Mexican Peso

Crisis, and the 1997 Asian Financial Crisis. A sudden reversal in capital flows led to
crisis and ultimately recession in many countries. The following year, the Russian
financial crisis then took place, and fears grew in the United States about the wider
ramifications, as the hedge fund Long-Term Capital Management (LTCM) came
under pressure. Indeed, this period saw 75bps of pre-emptive rate cuts from the
Federal Reserve.
This volatility also saw the Fed proactively seek to step in and smooth out the

economic volatility. This was broadly successful, but in the process also helped
engender complacency and a further buildup of leverage. Indeed, after the dot com
bubble burst, the federal funds rate was cut to just 1% in mid-2003, where it
remained until mid-2004.
This period saw clear optimism that another Great Depression was highly unlikely.
For instance, Nobel Prize-winning economist Robert Lucas argued in his 2003 AEA
Presidential Address that “macroeconomics in this original sense has succeeded:

Its central problem of depression prevention has been solved, for all practical
purposes, and has in fact been solved for many decades.”
However, as we now know, the 2008 financial crisis occurred, driving the biggest
global downturn since the Great Depression of the 1930s. It turned out that Minsky
had been correct, and a lengthy period without a crisis had once again helped to
induce complacency, laying the foundations for a repeat.

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

Section 3: The Post-2008 world: Why have we not had
another big financial crisis?
Since the Global Financial Crisis of 2008, we have thankfully avoided a major
financial crisis. Despite clear moments of stress, most notably around the collapse
of SVB in March 2023, we have not seen a similar wave of bank collapses on that
scale. There are several components to this.
First, post-GFC deleveraging cleared out risks in the system, with countries and
sectors that had excess indebtedness before the GFC experiencing pronounced

declines in leverage.
Second, there have been reforms explicitly designed to avoid a repeat of 2008.
After the crisis happened, there was a clear recognition that gaps in regulation had
exacerbated the crisis, leading to effects that cascaded across the financial system.
For instance, the Basel III reforms required banks to hold more equity, with a
minimum requirement for Common Equity Tier 1 (CET1) capital. They also
introduced the liquidity coverage ratio, which requires banks to hold enough high-

quality liquid assets to ensure they can cover their net cash outflows under a 30-day
stressed scenario. In addition, the net stable funding ratio was brought in, requiring
banks to have sufficient stable funding that exceeded the required amount of stable
funding for a one-year stressed scenario.
At the national level, further reforms were also introduced. In the US, the Dodd-
Frank Act was introduced, with measures including the Volcker Rule that restricted

banks from engaging in proprietary trading, and required the Federal Reserve to
perform an annual stress test of large institutions. Meanwhile in the European
Union, which also experienced a sovereign debt crisis, reforms included a “Single
Supervisory Mechanism”, whereby banking supervision was brought under the
ECB, ensuring harmonised regulatory standards.

### Page 8

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 8 Deutsche Bank AG
Third, the share of financial assets held by banks has fallen since the 2008 crisis.
This hasn’t eliminated risk, but commercial banks are funded by retail deposits that
can quickly fly. By contrast, non-bank financial institutions (NBFIs), e.g. hedge
funds, are not as prone to these bank run dynamics. For instance, a private credit
closed-ended fund is not one where investors can simply redeem their funds on

demand.
We can see this shift in the data. Looking at the FSB’s group of 21 plus the Eurozone,
we can see that the share of assets held by banks has fallen from a peak of 46% in
2008, to 38% in 2024. By contrast, the share held by NBFIs has risen from 43% to
51%.
Figure 5: Share of Assets of Financial Corporations (FSB 21 + EA Group)
35%

45%

Banks Nonbank Financial Intermediation Sector (NBFI)
Source :Haver Analytics, Deutsche Bank

Fourth, the lessons of 2008 have meant that when the financial system has had
wobbles since, policymakers have acted with incredible speed to stop the
turmoil.
This was most obvious when the Covid-19 pandemic began. Whilst a pandemic
was very different to a financial crisis, there was initially huge turmoil in financial
markets. Indeed, the S&P 500 fell by more than a third in just over a month, a decline
at a speed and scale unseen since the Great Depression. Meanwhile, US HY credit

spreads soared to levels last seen around the financial crisis, and at the height of the
turmoil, there was a huge spike in real yields, despite the economy entering a
recession.
Against this backdrop, central banks wasted no time in acting. For instance, the Fed
delivered an emergency 50bp rate cut, followed by another 100bp rate cut.
Moreover, they swiftly resumed Quantitative Easing, and announced the
establishment of multiple new facilities to ensure the smooth functioning of credit

markets. These included a Primary Market Corporate Credit Facility, to support new
bond and loan issuance, along with a Secondary Market Corporate Credit facility,
which offered liquidity for outstanding corporate bonds. Meanwhile, a third facility,
called the Term Asset-Backed Securities Loan Facility, was set up to enable the
issuance of asset-backed securities. Similarly in Europe, the ECB set up a new
Pandemic Emergency Purchase Programme (PEPP), which ultimately had a total

envelope of €1.85tn.
Whilst the pandemic caused a huge economic shock, the swift action meant it did
not translate into a financial one, and the brief tightening in credit conditions was

### Page 9

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 9
unwound over the coming months.
A similar pattern was seen during the regional banking turmoil of March 2023. This
was a financial panic, which initially began with the collapse of Silicon Valley Bank.
However, this began to spread, and Signature Bank also failed. Then in Europe,
Credit Suisse came under investor scrutiny and saw large deposit outflows, which
culminated in a purchase by UBS that included guarantees from the Swiss

government. This coincided with huge market moves, with the 2yr Treasury yield
posting its biggest daily decline in over 40 years, with a 61bps decline on March 13.
However, policymakers swiftly moved to stop any contagion. The US Treasury,
Federal Reserve and the FDIC released a joint statement stating that all deposits at
SVB would be protected, and that the Federal Reserve would provide additional
funding to ensure banks could meet the needs of depositors. Meanwhile, the Fed’s

balance sheet briefly expanded again, despite the fact it had begun the process of
quantitative tightening. These actions ultimately meant the crisis proved contained
and did not lead to a wave of further bank collapses in the manner of 2008.

Fifth, policymakers have also responded to recent shocks with huge fiscal

transfers, meaning that debt has migrated from private towards sovereign
balance sheets.
Whilst global debt has risen slightly over the last decade, if you dig deeper into the
numbers, that’s entirely driven by a rise in government debt. If we compare the
statistics for Q2 2026, relative to Q2 2016, there’s actually been a decline in debt for
households, financial corporations, and nonfinancial corporations. The only one of
the four segments that’s gone up in the last decade is government debt.

In relative terms, the transformation is even bigger if we look back to 2007, before
the Global Financial Crisis kicked off. Back then, government debt was the smallest
of the four categories, but because of the GFC and the pandemic, it’s now become
the biggest of the four categories.
That in turn has led to separate issues, with government bond yields at multi-year
highs, alongside rising net interest spending. So the fiscal space has become
limited. However, public balance sheets don’t face the same kind of system-wide

run that a bank would.

Overall, the post-2008 experience offers three key forward-looking lessons. First,
stronger bank balance sheets and the shift towards non-bank finance suggest

future stress may originate outside banks. As such, historical analyses centred on
banking crises may provide a less complete guide. Second, policymakers have
become increasingly effective at containing liquidity pressures and amplification.
And third, the shift in debt towards sovereign balance sheets has cushioned private-
sector shocks but narrowed the capacity for future intervention.
Section 4: What are potential candidates for the next crisis?

This section considers some of the current risks in the financial system and whether
they could be sowing the seeds for the next financial crisis. This section is not
designed to be a comprehensive list of financial risks, but rather an assessment of
three important areas where risks have risen in recent years.
1. Could AI spark broader vulnerabilities?
A huge driver of the current equity bull run has been the growth in AI-related stocks.
This report won’t explore the debate as to whether this is a bubble, but examine

whether a correction could eventually propagate into a systemic financial crisis. As
noted earlier, a reversal in the investment cycle need not cause a crisis in itself, and
the bursting of the dot com bubble also did not lead to a broader financial crisis.
Nevertheless, there are issues that could spark vulnerabilities. First, the rapid rise
in AI capex raises questions about whether the investment will pay off. The AI build-
out is on course to exceed the scale of past technological investment cycles, which

have often involved overinvestment even when the technologies ultimately proved
transformative. Second, funding is shifting from predominantly operating cash
flow towards external capital, and rising debt issuance in particular. Third, greater
equity-market concentration increases exposure to a reassessment of technology
valuations. Together, these developments make a correction a clear risk.
To assess the potential for systemic stress, we apply the framework in Greenwood,

Hanson, Shleifer and Sørensen (GHSS). This associates heightened crisis risk with
the combination of a strong run-up in asset prices and excessive credit growth,
estimating a 45% probability of a crisis within 3 years if the two thresholds in Figure
11 are met. On a backward-looking basis as of 2025, an economy-wide assessment
finds that the equity-price increase meets the framework’s “red zone” test, but
business credit growth has been considerably more subdued, given the slowdown

### Page 11

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 11
in credit following the 2022-23 tightening cycle. That is, going into 2026, the pattern
was one of a displacement of credit from other interest-sensitive sectors towards
AI and technology rather than any broad-based rise in leverage.
Nevertheless, US data does show a clear acceleration in credit trends so far in 2026.
To make a more forward-looking assessment, we incorporate the rise in issuance

in 2026 and indicatively extend it into 2027 using the estimated rise in AI capex
plans, assuming credit growth elsewhere in the US economy remains stable
(footnote: We see two-sided risks to this – we may understate the broader credit
demand in tech adjacent areas; conversely, this may understate the drag of higher
rates on credit demand in non-AI related sectors). We also extend the estimated
2027 trend into 2028, including an assumption that equity prices continue their

trend over the past 12 months, to provide an illustrative scenario over the next two
years. Under these assumptions, we would see a visible rise in aggregate US
business sector leverage, leaving the sector just shy of economy-wide red-zone
levels by 2028. As such, approaching that territory by 2028 is conceivable if the AI
cycle continues to accelerate, both in terms of increased debt borrowing and a
renewed rise in valuations.
While the GHSS framework gives a useful quantification of the potential risks, its

indicators also warrant some qualification, which we judge to come in opposing
directions:
n On the one hand, there is a risk that aggregate credit measures may
understate vulnerabilities when borrowing is highly concentrated. Circular
financing, leases and off-balance-sheet structures complicate the picture,
and while we incorporate the recent rise in payables in our leverage metric
to capture some of this risk, it may well be underestimated. Moreover,

realised interest costs remain below current market borrowing rates after
the lengthy period of ultra-low rates, so leverage trends alone may miss the
pressure from rising debt-service costs.
n On the other hand, the price-based metric used by GHSS to assess excess
asset price growth (the 3-year real rise in equity prices) may overstate
valuation excesses given the strong rise in earnings. Indeed, P/E multiples
have already seen a sizeable correction since late 2025, which was most

pronounced for software stocks, and currently there is little evidence of
overly stretched tech valuations, especially when compared to the dot com
boom.
The risk of stress amplification is also likely to depend on the nature of any AI
correction. A downgrading of the AI capex cycle due to slower growth in AI
demand, financing constraints or supply bottlenecks may result in weaker
valuations, leading to negative wealth effects and slower capital expenditure.

However, while this would create an economic headwind, it need not produce a
systemic crisis. And while parallels to the dot com bubble may well be relevant,
currently there are factors that suggest downside risks would be more moderate.
For instance, operating cash flow still plays an important role in financing the AI
buildout, whilst there has been a limited run-up in borrowing and much less
stretched valuations relative to the late-1990s.
Our assessment therefore remains that an AI correction is a clearer risk than an AI-

led systemic crisis at this point. For these risks to become more material, it would
need leverage to keep building but AI returns to eventually disappoint, raising the
likelihood of credit events and amplifying the investment slowdown through the
real economy. We will focus on this interaction between debt-service pressures and

### Page 12

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 12 Deutsche Bank AG
the role of credit events more in the next section.

2. Risks from higher rates and private credit
In the previous section we noted that modest economy-wide debt growth doesn’t
necessarily imply modest credit risk when debt-service costs are rising. As existing
debt reprices or is refinanced, borrowers can face greater pressure even without
additional borrowing. This is a key feature of the debt cycle, whereby vulnerabilities
accumulate during the expansion, and rising rates (often amid a monetary

tightening due to an overheating economy) can then expose them. These risks are
relevant in the context of the current rate hiking cycle, especially as much of the
catch up in debt service costs is yet to play out (see chart below).
With banking-sector risks having moderated since the GFC, the rapid expansion of
private credit and its opaque structure have understandably drawn attention as a
possible source of the next crisis. Parallels with the subprime credit boom have

been periodically made. However, we think these should not be overstated. Even
though the dollar size of the US private credit market is similar to that of subprime
around 2006-07, the market must be assessed relative to the economy (which has
doubled in nominal terms). Moreover, the slower build-up, restrictions on
redemptions and lower risks of direct wealth effects all suggest much more
moderate amplification risks than in 2008.
There are already signs that the private-credit growth cycle is slowing (see chart)

and our credit strategists have noted warning signs including weaker new inflows
into BDCs, increasing payment-in-kind interest and prospective refinancing
pressures. That said, defaults remain limited thus far so these should not be seen
as evidence of an imminent systemic event.

### Page 14

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 14 Deutsche Bank AG
Whether more systemic risks could emerge will depend on issues of solvency,
opacity and capacity for intervention.
n First, while redemption gates can limit immediate cash outflows and
reduce the need to sell illiquid loans under pressure, they do not restore a
borrower’s ability to service debt or absorb underlying credit losses. This
distinction between liquidity protection and solvency is therefore central to

judging whether a slowing credit cycle remains orderly.
n Opacity remains a concern, both in financing structures and in the
distribution of exposures among end-holders, including insurers and other
non-banks. This can make losses harder to identify and potential
amplification harder to assess.
n Policymakers’ success in containing liquidity pressures may not translate
into an effective response to credit events - liquidity provision alone cannot

repair impaired balance sheets. Extending support to opaque non-bank
exposures raises difficult questions about eligibility, loss allocation and the
threshold for intervention.
Quite aside from private credit, the fact that we are back in a rate hiking cycle from
the major central banks also adds to the current risks. Indeed, higher rates bring
down inflation, in part because they tighten financial conditions. But a
consequence of this is that it often reveals vulnerabilities in the financial system,

and makes a crisis more likely. For instance, it was a contributing factor behind the
2023 regional banking crisis, as banks had growing unrealised losses on their
securities portfolio when rates began to rise again. So even if higher rates don’t
directly cause a crisis, they can often be a key contributor. Indeed, hiking cycles
have regularly coincided with some kind of shock, as the experience of recent
decades demonstrates.

3. Stretched fiscal positions and possible NBFI amplifiers
As discussed earlier, the migration of debt towards sovereign balance sheets has

helped cushion private-sector shocks, but this has come at the cost of higher fiscal
risks. Higher public debt and sovereign yields have limited the room for fiscal
stimulus, while persistent inflation has limited the room for monetary stimulus. This
makes another round of coordinated macroeconomic support harder to deliver.
The issue with high public debt is that it if fiscal sustainability is questioned, that can
raise sovereign borrowing costs, which can amplify existing private-sector

vulnerabilities via a tightening in financial conditions and slower growth.
The financial risk posed by sovereign stress will also depend on the financial
amplification channels. These were clear in the European sovereign crisis with the
bank-sovereign loop, but the changing structure of sovereign markets suggests
that the sovereign–NBFI feedback loop may now warrant increasing attention. This
was visible with the 2022 LDI crisis in the UK, while a more recent concern is the run

up in leveraged hedge fund positions in sovereign bond markets, with hedge funds’
sovereign bond exposures more than doubling since 2022 (see chart below). As
noted also by the IMF, this reflects greater issuance and a rising share of sovereign
debt held by hedge funds.
The lessons from Black Monday, LTCM and the LDI crisis offer reassurance that
intervention can contain market stress originating outside the banking system. But
they are not a guarantee that future liquidity shocks will remain economically

benign. The greater scale and interconnectedness of non-bank finance mean that
we can’t simply rely on historical examples today.
Looking forward, a combination of sovereign repricing, leveraged financial
amplification and a more constrained policy response risks proving harder to
resolve than past episodes.

Conclusion
Whilst a major financial crisis has been avoided since the GFC and the subsequent
sovereign crisis, the problem of financial instability has yet to be permanently

solved. On the one hand, reforms to the financial system and the swift actions of
policymakers have helped to prevent a repeat. But as we know from Hyman
Minsky’s warning, lengthy periods of stability can often lay the foundations for the
next crisis, even if these take place slowly. Indeed, risks often shift rather than
disappear.
Looking forward, there are several vectors of risk worth paying attention to. But
financial crises often start in areas where people are paying little attention, or risks

are assumed to be contained. Indeed, it is the areas where risk is underappreciated
which often prove the most vulnerable.
As such, predicting where the next crisis might arise is inherently difficult. But the
topic is a vital one, given the long-lasting damage that financial crises can cause
when they do occur. Indeed, with the fiscal space for sovereigns increasingly
constrained today, the margin for a future policy error is limited.

3
《歐元投資關鍵-法國政治風險令歐元走弱》
10月以來歐元區一連串的動盪導致歐元走弱 。除了
西班牙提前大選 ，法國政治局勢惡化 ，極右派勝出
機率上升加學生示威遊行演變成各地零星暴動 ，經
濟下行風險升溫 。法國政府提出目標將赤字占 GDP
比從5.4%降至5%的方案 ，但反對黨已表態不支持 。
目前執政黨在下議院非多數 ，財政政策停擺風險導
致公債殖利率大幅上升 。法國10年期公債殖利率與
德國公債殖利率利差創2011年歐債危機最嚴重時期
以來最高水準 。本行認為 ，法國右翼已放棄脫歐 ，

且強調財政紀律將遵守歐盟規範 ，法國選後議會仍
需籌組多數聯盟，財政惡化風險可控。
總經方面 ，歐元區經濟基本面持續轉佳 。10月PMI
顯示經濟持續擴張，出口訂單連續第3個月增加，顯
示外需逐步改善 。10月PMI對應歐元區GDP季增率
約0.3%，為近3年相對穩健的季度表現 。通膨方面 ，
能源成本持續上升 ，企業為維持獲利而調升售價 ，
高利率逐漸抑制需求 。德國央行最新通膨報告顯示 ，
目前複製 2021-22年高通膨階段的可能性不大 ，但
通膨風險明顯偏向上行。展望後續，ECB 10月會議
維持利率不變 ，但通膨的僵固性將支持 12月升息，
歐洲與美利差預期小幅收斂 。基本面繼續支持 ECB
升息的環境下 ，預計美歐利差收斂將提供歐元支撐 ，
短線政治風險提供逢低布局時機，4Q26目標價1.19。

《英鎊投資關鍵-英國經濟動能逐漸改善》

居高不下 ，使英國央行將維持在貨幣緊縮的路徑 ，

景逐漸樂觀，英鎊兌美元年底目標1.34。

《瑞郎投資關鍵-瑞士景氣連續第7個月擴張》
瑞士9月製造業PMI下滑1.8點至55.3，但連續第7個
月維持在擴張區間。本次下滑主因產出、供應商交貨
時間及就業分項走弱。訂單積壓指數則持穩於57.0，
顯示需求仍具韌性，但企業持續去化庫存，原材料與
成品庫存皆低於50。採購價格指數升至70，反映電
子零組件及原物料價格上漲，中東地緣政治風險及AI
熱潮亦推升成本壓力。服務業PMI由58.3升至59.8，
維持強勁成長，新訂單與商業活動改善，就業指數升
至2025年4月以來高點。採購價格及銷售價格同步明
顯上升，顯示服務業通膨壓力有增溫跡象。整體而言，

瑞士通膨及成長前景溫和，央行並未因地緣政治風險
而強調通膨壓力，利差擴大為瑞郎走弱主因。央行對
升息態度保守，外需回溫與製造業改善正帶動瑞士經
濟展望持續好轉。4Q26美元兌瑞郎維持目標價0.8。

3Q26 反映漲價效應，營運顯著優於預期 
台光電 3Q26 營收 608.9 億元(季增 28.8%/年增 142.1%)，毛利率 37.7%，
季增 3.8ppts/年增 7.6ppts，優於預期的 35.1%，主因 1)已反應原料價格上
漲；2) 基礎設施約季增 32%為成長最強勁的產品線、行動裝置約季增 16%、
汽車 /工業 /其他約季增 25%，同時 M7 以上比重進一步提升至 65-70% 
(2Q26 為 60-65%)。營業利益 186.9 億元，季增 46.7%/年增 274.5%，優於
預期 21%；獲利方面，業外包含補助款 1.07 億元、利息收入、匯兌等貢獻，
整體 3Q26 稅後獲利 145 億元，季增 46.9%/年增 265.7%，EPS 40.47 元。 
4Q26 將反映下一輪漲價，同時高階 CCL 將更為吃緊 
展望 4Q26/1Q27，本中心認為隨高階 CCL 吃緊、原物料價格進一步上揚，
估計台光電將以此營運基礎更向上，同時預計 N 公司、ASIC 等新一代產品
開始量產，預計台光電產能將持續維持滿載，並預計崑山廠擴產進度往前，
預計高階 CCL 10 萬張提前供應，1Q27 也將有崑山跟中山新產能開出，本
中心分別上調 4Q26/1Q27 營收 23%/15% 至 717.4/769.4 億元 ( 季增
17.8%/7.3%)，毛利率 39.1%/39.8%，營業利益 231.9 億元/253.9 億元，季
增 24.1%/9.5%，EPS 上修 34%/23%至 50.1/53.68 元，季增 23.8%/9%。 
台光電新產能持續開出，整體成長趨勢明確，維持買進 
展望 2026/27 年，產能仍滿載、訂單能見度更長、傳輸規格升級明確，預期
產品組合將持續提升，估計 M7 比重將提升至 75-80%，本中心上修 EPS 
18%/18%至 133.02/268.89 元。台光電整體成長趨勢明確，同時也看到公司
積極準備後續擴產所需的原料，預期後續高階 CCL 效益將更為顯現，儘管
短期觀音廠因土建及驗收而延遲，不過台光 1Q27-2Q27 仍有新產能持續開
出，得以因應客戶強勁需求。本中心維持 27 倍本益比並以 2027 年 EPS 進
行評價，推得目標價 7,260 元，維持「買進」評等。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 營收 608.9 億元，毛利率 37.7%，優於預期的 35.1%，主因漲價
效應及 M7 以上產品比重提升至 65–70%。 
⧫

崑山新產能提前開出，上修 4Q26/1Q27 EPS 至 50.10/53.68 元。 
⧫  
需求持續強勁，預估 M7 以上產品比重將升至 75–80%；上修 2026/27
年 EPS 至 133.02/268.89 元，目標價 7,260 元，維持買進評等。

4Q26 將反映下一輪漲價，同時高階 CCL 將更為吃緊

元，季增 24.1%/9.5%，稅後獲利 179.5 億元/192.3 億元，EPS 分別上修 34%/23%至 50.1/53.68
元，季增 23.8%/9%。

現高階 CCL 產能供不應求，台光電規劃至 2030 年的產能 
目前台光電為具備高階 CCL 產能的廠商，目前已觀察到高階 CCL 需求持續強勁，同時客戶目前給
予訂單能見度拉長，現台光電規劃 2030 年產能將達 1525 萬張，叫 2026 年的 585 萬張成長 160%：
以台光電最新的各年度產能規劃如下，1) 原定 12M26 將開出的觀音廠產能因土建及驗收延遲，因
此遞延至 2H27 方能開出，且原訂後續的 60 萬張則遞延至 2029 年，2026 年總產能將為 585 萬張；
2) 2027 年規劃為 900 萬張產能：1Q27 將加入崑山 50 萬張新產能、中山 60 萬張、2Q27 檳城 15
萬張、此外中山廠 60 萬張、觀音廠 60 萬張、黃石 30 萬張、觀音廠 30 萬張將於 2027 年底完成擴
充；3) 2028 年將加入崑山 55 萬張、中山 90 萬張產能，總產能共計 1045 萬張；同時隨觀音、中
山、檳城進一步擴產，2029/2030 年產能將達 1195 萬張/1525 萬張，體產能擴充趨勢明確，且目
前台光電已為全球最大的 CCL 廠商，具備最全面的客戶群及高階 CCL 產能，台光電龍頭地位穩固。

高階 CCL 趨勢不變，預期 M7 以上比重持續增加 
展望 2026/2027 年，整體 CCL 廠商產能仍趨於滿載，客戶洽談訂單能見度更長，以目前產能方不
足以滿足客戶需求，主因 AI Server、Switch、General Server，皆已見需求浮現，傳輸規格升級越
加明確，AI Server 方面也將隨新一代陸續往 M8+/M9 規格升級，預期產品組合將持續提升，同時
美系網通廠也進一步指出 1.6T 將為 2027 年最大成長動能並優於 800G 出或，整體 Switch 需求將
隨 Scale up 應用而明顯提速，估計台光電 M7 比重將陸續提升至 75-80%，綜合上述，本中心就此
次財報調整 2026/2027 年 財 務 預 估 ， 預 估 2026/2027 年 營 收 2129.6/3665.1 億 元 ， 年 增
125.9%/72.1%，上修幅度 10%/11%，毛利率 36%/40%，營業利益 617.4/1249.8 億元，年增
223.1%/102.4%，稅後獲利 476.6/963.5 億元，年增 225.4%/102.1%，EPS 133.02/268.89 元，上
修幅度 18%/18%。

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 29 日 Elite Material 第4 頁，共 26頁  
產品結構分析  
台光電 AI 伺服器與高速網通帶動基礎設施營收占比提升 
台光電主要供應銅箔基板（CCL）與黏合片（Prepreg），為 PCB 製造所需之基礎材料，產品涵蓋
不同損耗等級，應用由智慧型手機延伸至 AI 伺服器、雲端資料中心、高速網通及車用電子。受惠
AI 伺服器及高速網通需求成長，基礎設施業務 (包含 AI 伺服器、高速網通、衛星 )占營收比重由
3Q25 的 65–70%提升至 3Q26 的 75–80%，同期行動裝置及車用／工業／其他業務預估占比分別
為 10–15%及 5–10%。3Q26 基礎設施營收季增 32%、年增 176%，台光電成長主要來自產品漲價
與高階材料出貨組合改善，亦估計帶動 M7 以上材料營收占比由 1Q26 的 55–60%提升至 3Q26 的
65–70%。

圖 3：基礎建設業務項目(F)

圖 4：基礎設施營收占比持續提升

資料來源：元大投顧預估 資料來源：公司資料、元大投顧預估

高速與低 CTE 材料並進，產品布局延伸至載板及 Physical AI 
隨 AI ASIC 及 1.6T 交換器升級，M8 產品 EM-892K2(目前需求以 1.6T Switch 為主)於 2H26 放量，
2027 年需求可望進一步增溫；M9 產品 EM-896 系列已於 3Q26 小量量產，相關產品多採 HDI 及至
少四次壓合設計且除追求更低 Df 外，也保留較低 CTE 及較高剛性，預期 2027 年將顯著貢獻；
M10 產品 EM-898K4 採 Q-Glass 方案，Df 降至 0.0004，預計 2027 年有望小量出貨。材料發展除
追求低損耗外，更重視低 CTE、翹曲控制及 HDI 加工良率，並透過 Hybrid 混壓結合高速與載板材
料，兼顧成本、電性及可靠度。載板材料方面，EM-S572T 採 T-Glass，具超低 CTE 約 0.8ppm/°C
及 40GPa 高模數，目前開始延伸至 CSP 等載板應用，4Q26 已開始量產。此外，台光電透過 A 系
列產品服務電動車、工業與人形機器人等 Physical AI 應用。原料方面，銅箔及玻纖布供應有限，
公司優先配置予 M8 以上產品。同時台光電也已規劃未來至 2030 年的產能將達 1525 萬張，後續
新增產能以 M8 以上為主。本中心正向看待高階 CCL 升級、載板材料滲透及產能擴張帶動 2027 年
營運成長。

### Page 5

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 29 日 Elite Material 第5 頁，共 26頁  
圖 5：台光電基礎設施業務產品

資料來源：公司資料

NV 與 ASIC 平台同步升規，成長動能由 M8／M8+放量延伸至 M9 導入 
GB300 的 Compute Tray 主要採用斗山 M8／M4 材料，台光電與生益科技為 Switch Tray 的 CCL
主要供應鏈；隨平台演進至 VR200，Switch Tray 材料往 M8+（M8.5）升級，PCB 層數由約 24 層
提高至 32 層，並新增約 44 層 Midplane，預計使用 M8 材料，高階材料占比逐漸提高。公司 M8+
材料於 2H26 逐步放量。M9 應用亦延伸至 UBB、LPU 及部分周邊板件，台光電預計為 LPU 之 M8
／M9 材料供應商之一。 
ASIC 方面，Google TPU 材料由 M7 往 M8／M8+提升，Sunfish／Zebrafish 進一步採用不同等級
材料搭配的 N+M 結構，後續 Humufish 也預計規劃往 HDI 發展，材料升級同時伴隨 PCB 結構與加
工複雜度提高。AWS Trainium 3 依氣冷與水冷方案分別採約 26 層及 40 層 PCB，使用 M8 級高速
材料；Meta 相關 ASIC 亦採用 M8 級材料，台光電均已切入上述供應鏈，在 Google 及 AWS 相關
平台預計為主要供應商，台光電持續受惠 TPU v9/Trainium 4 的 CCL 規格持續升級，加上 AMD、
Meta 等客戶或相關專案提供額外成長動能。

同業比較分析  
台廠皆陸續推出高階材料，規格升級仍為 CCL 產業趨勢 
台光電 M8 主力 EM-892K／K2 於 10GHz 下的 Df 分別為 0.0017／0.0013，M9 的 EM-896K2／K3
則進一步降至 0.0009／0.0008；就同業高階料號而言，台燿 TU-953R／Q 為 0.0008／0.0006、聯
茂 IT-999GSE2／GSE3 為 0.0009／0.0007，南亞 NPG-999K／K2／Q 則為 0.00083／0.0007／
0.00045，各家均已建立 Df 低於 0.001 的產品選項。進入 M10 後，台光電 EM-898K4 與聯茂 IT-
Q10GSE4 的 Df 均約 0.0004，台燿 HC 系統 TU-963F／Q 分別為 0.00036／0.0004，南亞 NPG-
999Q 則為 0.00045，電性數據已落在相近區間。

台光以 Q 布方案為主、台燿並行 HC 與 PTFE 方案、南亞以自有材料拓展高階產
品 
在下一代材料路線上，台燿 TU-963Q 採 HC 碳氫樹脂搭配石英布、TU-963F 為 HC 無布方案，另
有 PTFE 無布產品 TU-973F，並提出 TU-973 作為 Core、TU-963 作為 Prepreg 的混壓設計，在電
性之外兼顧多層板壓合及加工需求；不過，無布材料的機械支撐與 PTFE 多層板加工性，仍須依實
際疊構與製程驗證。台光電 M10 主要以碳氫樹脂材料配合 Q Glass 為主，聯茂 IT-Q10GSE4 則採
HC 樹脂、Q 布及 HVLP4／5 銅箔，南亞則搭配自行開發的 M9 樹脂，並整合 Low-Dk 玻纖布與
HVLP 銅箔，持續推進高階 CCL 產品。

台光電高階高速材料占比領先同業，優先受惠規格升級 
應用佔比方面 ：台光電營收結構持續向 AI 基礎設施集中， 3Q26 產品組合: 基礎設施營收占比由
3Q25 的 65–70%提升至 3Q26 的 75–80% ( 其中 AI 伺服器估計占基礎設施營收 55–60%、一般伺
服器與交換器各占 15–20%、低軌衛星占 5–10%)、Mobile device 約 10%～15%、汽車、工業與其
他合計約 5%～15%。聯茂 2Q26 應用別營收佔比為基礎建設 73% ( 其中伺服器占 80–85%，但需
求仍以一般伺服器為主)、車用電子 14%、智慧型手機 5%、消費性電子 8%。台光電 AI 伺服器約
占整體營收四至五成，已成為主要營運重心且為 AI 伺服器佔比較高的台廠。 
材料等級方面：台光電 M7 以上營收占比估計由 1Q26 的 55–60%逐步提高 2Q26 的 60–65%、
3Q26 65 -70%，應用涵蓋 GPU 伺服器 Switch Tray 、ASIC 加速器板及高速交換器。台燿 3Q26 材
料等級營收占比: ELL ／SLL（約對應 M7／M8）38% 主要受惠 A 客戶 ASIC 及美系 C 客戶高速網
通需求以及衛星相關應用； VLL／LL（約對應 M4／M6）28% 主要應用於傳統配板、 PCIe／
General Server 及 Telecom；HDI／N-LL 19%；Others 15% 主要對應 Power 與厚銅材料。聯茂則
仍以 M4 以下產品為主，比重約 70%，短期較受惠中低階材料漲價，後續升級動能包括 Intel Oak 
Stream、AMD Venice 等一般伺服器平台導入 PCIe Gen6 ，帶動材料由 M6 轉向 M7，以及美系
GPU 加速器導入 M9。南亞 M 系列占 CCL 營收比重則由 2Q26 不到 20%，現已提高至約 30%，惟
其中 M4 仍占逾半，廣泛應用於消費性電子與一般伺服器，其餘規格又以 M6 最多，顯示現階段產
品結構仍偏向中低階材料。本中心認為，台光電目前高階高速材料營收比重較高，且已廣泛參與
GPU、ASIC 與高速交換器等應用，為 AI 基礎設施材料規格升級的優先受惠廠商。

台光電擴產規模領先，新增產能聚焦 M8 以上高階材料 
台光電持續擴充全球產能，月產能預計由 2026 年底 585 萬張，提升至 2027 年底 900 萬張、2028
年底 1,045 萬張、2029 1,195 萬張、2030 1,525 萬張，新增產能分布於台灣觀音／桃園，以及崑
山、中山、黃石與檳城等廠區，且新增產能將以 M8 以上高階材料為主，配合 2026 年新專案多由
M8 起跳，預期新增產能的產品組合將高於既有平均。同業方面，台燿 2027 年擴產主要來自泰國
與常熟，後續持續擴充泰國及中山廠，2026 至 2029 年底月產能預計分別達 260 萬張、380 萬張、
530 萬張及 575 萬張，並透過泰國布局承接客戶海外生產需求。聯茂則以無錫與泰國為擴產重心，
其中無錫新廠定位為 M7 以上高階高速材料生產基地，全球月產能預計由 2026 年底 570 萬張，提
升至 2027 年底 630 萬張、2028 年底 825 萬張及 2029 年底 915 萬張。以 2026 年底至 2028 年底
的新增產能觀察，台光電兼具較大的擴產規模與 M8 以上材料配置，有利於承接 AI 伺服器、ASIC
及高速交換器需求，進一步鞏固高階 CCL 供應優勢。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 29 日 Elite Material 第11 頁，共 26頁  
財務分析  
台光電營收獲利持續成長，各項財務指標穩健 
台光電 2021–2025 年流動比率與速動比率分別維持於約 146–178%及 117–141%，2025 年分別為
160.0%及 122.4%，顯示短期流動性具一定緩衝。負債比率則由 2021 年的 45.9%升至 2024 年的
53.9%，2025 年小幅降至 53.3%，反映擴產及營運規模擴大下的資金需求。2022 年及 2024 年負
債增加與發行可轉換公司債有關。 
獲利能力方面，ROE 由 2021 年的 30.1%降至 2022／2023 年的 24.3%／22.5%，2022 年手機與
筆電相關出貨減少，雖由基礎設施產品成長支撐，全年營收仍僅微增 0.45%，營運成長受到消費性
電子需求疲弱限制，合併稅後淨利減少 7.7%且可轉債轉換增加導致權益增加較多。後續回升至
2024／2025 年的 30.9%／34.3%。主因 2024–2025 年公司則受惠有利產品組合，帶動 ROE 連續
回升，股東資金報酬率隨營運改善而提升。 
經營能力方面，應收帳款收現天數由 2021 年的 110.0 天延長至 2023 年的 128.2 天，後續回落至
2025 年的 120.1 天；應付帳款付現天數則由 2021 年的 89.7 天延長至 2025 年的 111.7 天。存貨週
轉天數於 2021–2024 年維持約 59–63 天，2025 年升至 72.3 天，存貨及應收帳款金額增加主要與
銷售規模持續擴大有關。儘管 2025 年庫存天數上升，收現天數下降及付款天數延長抵銷了大部分
增加的資金占用，使現金轉換週期由 2024 年的 79.8 天小幅增至 2025 年的 80.6 天，整體營運資金
週轉尚未明顯惡化。 
利息保障倍數由 2021 年的 91.6 倍降至 2022／2023 年的 35.2 倍／24.3 倍， 2024–2025 年隨產品
組合改善及獲利成長，帶動利息保障倍數回升至 27.4 倍及 40.7 倍。2021-2025 低點仍逾 24 倍，
顯示獲利對利息費用具有相當覆蓋能力，擴產期間的償債能力仍有營運獲利支撐。

### Page 12

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 29 日 Elite Material 第12 頁，共 26頁  
產業概況  
AI、ASIC、通用型強勢出擊，2026 年美系 CSP 資本支出估年增 91%  
在 ChatGPT 等 AI 應用陸續推向市場，各大業者紛紛開始研發自有超級大模型，包括 Gemini 
(Google)、Claude (Anthropic) 、Llama (Meta) 等，各大品牌開始大舉投入大型語言模型(LLM)市場，
且每一個產品的智慧皆持續提高，部分消費者也陸續從 ChatGPT 陣營轉移至 Gemini 陣營，反映
出消費者開始逐漸提升對 AI 的黏著度。根據 Grand View Research 調查，2024 年全球 AI 市場營
收約 2,792 億美元，預估 2030 年市場規模超過 3 兆美元，擁有超過 30% 的年複合增長率。 
面對這爆發性的長期成長趨勢，CSP 業者(Amazon、Microsoft、Google、Meta)在法說會上一致強
調，AI 投資已轉變為一項橫跨 2026 年的結構性戰略，CSP 大廠不僅持續擴大對 GB300 等次世代
AI 伺服器的採購，更將重點放在資料中心基礎設施的長期戰略規劃，以應對 AI 運算對電力及散熱
系統強勁成長的需求，確保未來數年內擁有足夠的能源與容量來支持持續迭代的大模型部署。本中
心認為，2026 年美國四大 CSP 資本支出將年增 91%，且將有一半的成長來自於資料中心建置，代
表了資料中心的不足及未來伺服器需求。明年不僅僅是 NVIDIA GB300 的天下，AWS、Google、
Meta ASIC 的崛起也將是一大關鍵，其中又以 Google TPU 需求最為強勁，已經有兩個客戶預計將
採購 TPU。 
另外，受惠於 AI 訓練與推論需求爆發的連帶效應，本中心預估 2026 年通用型伺服器出貨量將年增
23%，其中以存儲型伺服器需求最為強勁。隨著生成式 AI 應用普及和使用者數量暴增，CSP 等業
者必須不斷擴充容量，且不僅限於高階 GPU 伺服器，龐大的 AI 模型訓練和推論在使用狀態中將消
耗大量的數據(DATA)，使得存儲型伺服器的需求大增。

### Page 13

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 29 日 Elite Material 第13 頁，共 26頁  
ASIC 進入高速成長期，GPU 需求仍維持擴張 
從 AI 晶片預期出貨來看，GPU 與 ASIC 需求均持續成長，但 2026 年後 ASIC 成長動能明顯加速。
Nvidia GPU 出貨量預估由 2025 年 565.7 萬顆增至 2026 年 650 萬顆、2027 年 850 萬顆，年增
17%及 34%；AMD 亦由 2025 年 90 萬顆增至 2027 年 180 萬顆，顯示通用 GPU 算力需求仍維持
成長。 
相較之下，ASIC 受惠 CSP 加速自研晶片及導入規模擴大，總出貨量預估由 2025 年 380 萬顆大幅
增至 2026 年 725 萬顆、2027 年 1,360 萬顆，年增率達 91%及 88%，成長速度明顯高於 GPU。其
中 Google 仍為最大 ASIC 使用者， 2027 年用量預估達 800 萬顆，AWS 亦增至 320 萬顆，
Microsoft 及 Meta 同步擴大部署。我們認為，在資料中心電力逐漸成為算力擴張瓶頸下， CSP 將
更重視每瓦運算效能及整體運算成本， ASIC 針對特定運算工作的效率優勢有望推升滲透率，惟
GPU 憑藉通用性及軟體生態系優勢，仍將維持重要地位。

圖 16：GPU/ASIC 出貨量預估

資料來源：元大投顧

AI 伺服器規格及需求顯著提升，未來可見 PCB 往多層板升級 
隨著 AI 晶片(如 NVIDIA GPU 及 CSP ASIC) 不斷迭代，伺服器和網路設備的 PCB/CCL 規格呈現
爆 炸 性 增 長 ， 層 數 和 尺 寸 都 大 幅 攀 升 。 如NVIDIA 方 面 ， 隨 著 GPU 從 A100/H100 演 進 至 
GB200/GB300，再到下一代 Rubin 系列， PCB 層數持續增加， GB200/GB300 Compute Tray 
(Bianca Board) 採用 HDI 結構，層數約為 22 層(5+12+5 設計)，Rubin 系列預計層數將升級至 24-
26 層，材料也需從 M8 級升級至更高規格；CSP 方面，AWS 將往 26 層架構、Google 往 44 層、
Meta 也將往 38-40 層架構提升。往多層式架構升級，主因為應對 AI 晶片帶來的兩大核心挑戰：電
源完整性(PI)和訊號完整性(SI)，更高層數的 PCB 提供足夠的訊號走線空間、完整的電源與地平面，
以及穩定的回流路徑，以達成超高速訊號的阻抗控制、提升供電完整性與整體訊號品質。  
2026 年 ASIC 晶片量大增，材料升級為明顯趨勢 
2024 年開始 ASIC 需求逐步提升，主要來自 Google 及 AWS，帶動整體 AI 市場持續升溫。進入
2025 年，AWS Trainium 2 、Google TPU v6 的 ASIC 伺服器產品需求大幅提升，其中 AWS ASIC
晶片產量預計將達到 160 萬顆，下半年則會有新一代 ASIC 晶片推出，包括 Trainium 2.5 及 Meta 
MTIA 2 等。展望 2026 年，隨著 Meta MTIA 3 、AWS Trainium 3 及 Google TPU V7 等產品逐步
上市，隨高速需求帶動材料端持續升規，預期 CCL 將陸續由 M7 升級至 M8/M9，玻纖布升級至
LDK、LDK2、Q 布；銅箔也將升級至 HVLP3/4。

### Page 14

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 29 日 Elite Material 第14 頁，共 26頁
高階 CCL 對銅箔、玻纖布規格要求持續提高
CCL 的核心組成包括，銅箔 (占成本約 40–50%)、玻纖布(25–40%)以及樹脂(20–30%)，三者經過
高溫高壓的壓合後成為 PCB 的關鍵介電材料。其中銅箔負責導電，玻纖布提供結構補強，而樹脂
則扮演兩者的黏結與介電性能調控角色。隨著伺服器傳輸速度大幅提升，高頻高速訊號對材料的穩
定性、損耗控制與結構強度要求同步加嚴，使得上游銅箔與玻纖布的等級成為影響  CCL 廠競爭力
的核心關鍵。
銅箔方面，銅箔在 CCL 中最核心的角色是導電與訊號傳輸。高速 PCB 的線路愈來愈細小，訊號傳
輸速率愈高，對銅箔的三大要求也同步升級：1)更低粗糙度(Ra 需降至 0.5 μm 以下)，以降低界面
散射造成的插入損耗； 2)更高平整度，確保訊號完整性、減少阻抗； 3)更高可靠度，承受大面積
GPU/ASIC 封裝下的熱循環壓力。因此，新一代 AI 板材大量採用 HVLP3、HVLP4、甚至 HVLP5。

玻纖布方面，其最大要求在於提升機械強度與維持板材穩定度。 GPU/ASIC 封裝尺寸變大，使
PCB 在熱負載下更容易翹曲，因此玻纖布的 CTE(熱膨脹係數)也變得比以往更重要。傳統 E-Glass
難以應付高速與熱衝擊，因此材料開始升級至 Low Dk1、Low Dk2，再到 Q-Glass。這些高階布料
除介電損耗更低、散佈更均勻外，其低 CTE 特性可讓 PCB 更耐熱、更抗翹曲、更耐層間應力，是
支撐高階 CCL 的關鍵材料。
綜合而言，AI 應用推動的高階 CCL，不再只是樹脂與銅箔性能的疊加，而是上游原料(銅箔、玻纖
布)與下游 CCL 設計共同協作的結果。因此掌握高階銅箔與布料的供應能力，已成為  CCL 廠能否
在 AI 時代維持領先與擴大市占的關鍵。
圖 17：愈高階 CCL，Dk/Df 要求愈高
CCL Dk (介電常數) Df (介電損耗) 銅箔
M6 3.8 <0.006 HVLP2
M7 3.5 <0.005 HVLP3
M8 3.0 <0.0015 HVLP3/4

M9 <3.0 <0.0007 HVLP4/5
資料來源：元大投顧整理
Low Dk/Low Df 電子級玻纖布製程要求精細，並已見石英布規格
在高速運算與 AI 伺服器的要求下，玻纖布製造難度大幅提升。整體難點來自三大核心環節：抽紗、
編織與表面處理。首先在抽紗端，愈高階布 (如 Low Dk2 、Q-Glass)纖維直徑愈細、均質度要求愈
高，任何溫度或孔徑偏移都會影響  Dk/Df，使高階抽紗線的速度更慢、良率更低。其次在編織階段，
細纖維容易斷紗，張力需極度穩定，布面不得出現波紋、跳針或皺折，否則會造成  CCL 平整度下
降、訊號完整性受損，這使得高階布的織布速度必須明顯降低。第三是表面處理 (上膠)，需以矽烷
界面讓玻纖能與樹脂良好結合，而 Q 布則因其窯爐溫度較玻纖布高(需達 1,700–2,000 度)，鑽孔、
雷射加工、除膠等工藝製程步驟皆需重新設計，不過其熱膨脹係數低、 Dk/Df 明顯具備優勢，因此
未來 CCL 往 M9 以上規格提升時，已將 Q 布解決方案考量進來，如台光電 EM-896K3、台燿 TU-

953Q 等皆以 Q 布取代玻纖布。
相關供應廠商仍以日系 Nittobo、Asahi 為主，相關台廠包含富喬 (1815 TT ，未評等)、台玻(1802
TT，未評等)、南亞(1303 TT，未評等)、建榮(5340 TT，未評等)。

### Page 16

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 29 日 Elite Material 第16 頁，共 26頁  
2026 年 PCIe 從 5.0 升級至 6.0，帶動 Connector/Cable 用量及 ASP 增加 
因 Scale-up 僅暫時將規範定義出來，在 2027-2028 年才會真正落實，不過 2026 年開始加速滲透
至 PCIe 6.0 ，又 NVIDIA 曾在 GTC 表示，為了跟上兆參數模型的規模，必須升級整個堆疊，如
GPU、CPU、網路、記憶體，這也是為什麼打造下一代 Vera CPU ，配備先進記憶體與 PCIe/CXL 
架構，以消除未來 AI 工廠中的所有瓶頸。 
因此在更高的 CPU 效 率 及 記 憶 體 擴 展 下 ， 加 速PCIe (Peripheral Component Interconnect 
Express) 、 CXL (Compute Express Link) 於 Server 間的升級， PCIe 通訊對象包含
CPU→GPU/SSD/NIC 等裝置之間的資料交換，如前述，為提升整體效能， PCIe 規格往 5.0、6.0
升級 (圖 3)，6.0 以上版本調頻技術由 NRZ 改使用 PAM4 ( 由以往的 1bit 升級至 2 bit ，代表頻寬翻
倍) 使傳輸資料翻倍。 
隨著伺服器傳輸協定由 PCIe 5.0 演進至 PCIe 6.0 ，訊號傳輸速度翻倍，這也對 PCB 的核心基材銅
箔基板(CCL)提出了極其嚴苛的物理挑戰。PCIe 6.0 首次引入了 PAM4 調變技術，雖然在相同頻率
下頻寬翻倍，但其訊號電壓準位更窄，這意味著對訊號完整性的容錯率大幅下降。為了抑制隨頻率
升高而劇增的介電損耗，板材必須從 PCIe 5.0 所使用的 Very Low Loss (M6) 等級，全面升級至 
Ultra Low Loss (M7) 或甚至 Super Ultra Low Loss (M8)等級。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 29 日 Elite Material 第17 頁，共 26頁  
獲利調整與股票評價  
維持台光電買進評等，目標價 7,260 元 
展望 2026/2027 年，整體 CCL 廠商產能仍趨於滿載，客戶洽談訂單能見度更長，以目前產能 不足
以滿足客戶需求，主因 AI Server、Switch、General Server，皆已見需求浮現，傳輸規格升級越加
明確，AI Server 方面也將隨新一代陸續往 M8+/M9 規格升級，預期產品組合將持續提升，同時美
系網通廠也進一步指出 1.6T 將為 2027 年最大成長動能並優於 800G 出或，整體 Switch 需求將隨
Scale up 應用而明顯提速，估計台光電 M7 比重將陸續提升至 75-80%，綜合上述，本中心就此次
財 報 調 整 2026/2027 年 財 務 預 估 ， 預 估 2026/2027 年 營 收 2129.6/3665.1 億 元 ， 年 增
125.9%/72.1%，上修幅度 10%/14%，毛利率 36%/41%，營業利益 617.4/1249.8 億元，年增
223.1%/102.4%，稅後獲利 476.6/963.5 億元，年增 225.4%/102.1%，EPS 133.02/268.89 元，上
修幅度 18%/18%。 
評價方面，本中心認為台光電整體成長趨勢明確，同時也看到公司積極準備後續擴產所需的原料，
預期後續高階 CCL 效益將更為顯現，儘管短期觀音廠因土建及驗收而延遲，不過台光 1Q27-2Q27
仍有新產能持續開出，得以因應客戶強勁需求。本中心維持 27 倍本益比並以 2027 年 EPS 進行評
價，推得目標價 7,260 元，維持「買進」投資評等。

公司簡介  
台光電子材料股份有限公司成立於 1992 年，為專業 CCL ( 銅箔基板)製造商，據研調機構 Prismark 統計，2023 年台光電
CCL 總產值世界第一，市占率約 33%，顯示台光電耕耘高階 CCL 市場多年之成果。目前台光電擁有桃園廠、新竹廠、昆
山廠、中山廠及黃石廠共 5 座工廠， 2025 年底 CCL 產能約 555 萬張/月，預計 2026 年底總產能最終達 615 萬張/月，同時
也預計於 2027 年底達 930 萬張/月。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 台光電整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低度風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於領先的
位置，在科技硬體行業的公司中亦領先同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 該子行業的公司通常面臨最高風險，涉及重大 ESG 問題，即碳自有營運、商業道德和資源使
用。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：台光電的資訊揭露力度強，反映了其對投資者和公眾的高度責任感。該公司採取了
一些措施來管理與重大 ESG 問題相關的風險，但缺乏關鍵領域的政策和方案。儘管如此，該公司尚未捲入任何與 ESG
相關的重大爭議

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

重點摘要  
1. 3Q26 營收 US$26.4mn （新台幣 8.45 億元），QoQ +8.4% 、YoY 
+46.5% ；InP 基板與磊晶供應回穩， AOC 營收 QoQ +25.8% ，營
收比重由 2Q26 的 61%回升至 71%，帶動毛利率由 43.3% 回升至
47.0% ，營業利益率 19.3% ， EPS 1.33 元。產能利用 率超過
100% 
2. 4Q26 財測營收 US$30 -34mn 、毛利率 46-50% 、營業費用
US$7.6- 8.6mn ，動能來自 AOC 需求持續強勁及威力富新增產能。
200G PD 驗證已全數完成，已有客戶開始上量，導入第二家基板來
源後預期 2Q27 起有顯著營收貢獻；100mW CW Laser 依客戶回饋
調整，預計 3Q27 小批量、4Q27 大批量出貨 
3. 公司 2027 年 InP 基板已全數鎖定，變數在出口核准流程，非中國
基板來源預計 2028 年後才達有意義比例；威力富增資擴產與美國
廠無塵室擴充預計 2Q27 完成。 
結論 
我們認為 3Q26 基板供應回穩已使 AOC 出貨與毛利率重回正軌，
4Q26 財測中位數 US$32mn 隱含 QoQ 約 21% 成長，在稼動率超過
100% 、2027 年基板已鎖定下，短期營收動能明確。 2027 年的關鍵驗
證點在於 200G PD 於 2Q27 放量、100mW CW Laser 於 2H27 出貨，
以及擴產於 2Q27 如期完成；非中國基板供應仍需時間、出口核准可
能遞延，費用亦將隨 CW Laser 攤銷與 RSA 酬勞成本墊高。整體而
言，我們看好公司在 AI 資料中心光通訊需求擴張下，憑藉 InP PD 規格
優勢與產能布局延續穩定成長

經營環境與供應鏈策略  
 公司以美國為主要生產基地，認為未來若有政治面限制，對公司屬
正面 
 2Q26 的 InP 基板供應問題目前已趨穩定，但公司不認為未來必然
維持穩定，持續開拓不同基板來源、vendor 與生產基地，確保供應
再度受限時仍能應變並持續供貨 
 產能稼動率超過 100% ，供不應求； 11/3 董事會通過對威力富增
資，以增加產能並補齊設備，定位為美國以外的重要生產基地；美
國廠亦將擴充 cleanroom 並補齊瓶頸機台，預計 2Q27 完成

3Q26 財務表現  
 3Q26 營收新台幣 8.45 億元，QoQ 以美元計+8.37% 、以新台幣計
+9.7% ；YoY 以美元計+46.5% 、以新台幣計+55.4%

71%、其他 3%

200G PD 與 CW Laser 進度 
 200G PD ： 
 所有驗證已完成，已有 1 家客戶開始上量  
 原基板廠商自身產能提升不如預期（與出口管制無關），將增加
另一家廠商的同規格基板作為第二來源，並進行 incremental  
verification  
 預期 2Q27 起大批量出貨並有顯著營收貢獻  
 100mW CW Laser 依客戶回饋調整，未來半年聚焦可靠度驗證與客
戶送樣，預計 3Q27 小批量、 4Q27 大批量出貨； 70mW 仍在
sample 階段，廠內主力為 100mW ，70mW 由合作夥伴供應 
 100G PD 自 9M26 出貨回升， 4Q26 多個生產基地同時供貨，公司
對穩定供貨具信心；2026 年 100G PD 市占與 2025 年相近，2027
年雖可能因客戶調整供應鏈略受影響，但公司有信心至少維持市占

InP 基板供應與技術定位  
 2027 年基板已全數鎖定，基板並非公司直接採購，而是由磊晶廠
商以長約向基板廠商鎖量，並涉及 prepayment 條件；變數在政治
面，出口需逐筆核准，流程可能遞延，但 2027 年整體供應量無虞  
 2027 年中國以外的基板供應仍未達合理水準，仍需仰賴中國出
口，2028 年後可望明顯改善；限制出貨的是基板，磊晶、晶圓製
造與後段均非瓶頸，基板廠產能亦持續倍增，問題在政治面  
 基板價格持續上漲，材料成本會反映給客戶，惟調價速度有時跟不
上材料漲幅  
 競爭優勢在良率與效能，規格優於同業，可協助客戶在模組設計上
節省 component 成本；公司以 4 吋晶圓生產，同業多為 2-3 吋，
價格具競爭力  
 InP PD 不會被完全取代：SiGe PD 約可做到 200G ，模組設計較複
雜且受傳輸距離限制，400G PD 仍將以 InP 為主；客戶選擇公司首
重品質、交期與產能，價格為次要

QA 
 公司對市場與業務的展望？ 
 2026- 2027 年預期為相當穩定的成長  
 客戶端幾個主要 player 短期內不會有太大變化  
 即使美國祭出 FCC 限制，也不會隔天即實施，會有過渡期，各
方都預期此事會發生並已做適當布局，整體展望沒有太大變化  
 100mW CW Laser 的驗證與出貨進度？為何遞延？  
 100mW 進度有所遞延，原因是客戶回饋需要做調整

 200G PD 的認證進度與放量時間點？  
 200G PD 所有驗證都已完成，已有 1 家客戶開始要上量

國出口；2028 年以後相對 2027 年會有相當程度的改善 
 磊晶廠是否更換不便說明，目前合作的磊晶廠商都是長期夥伴  
 InP 基板的供應狀況與價格變化？ 
 價格往上，基板目前奇貨可居 
 2027 年的基板已全部鎖定，供貨不應有問題；變數在政治面，
出口需逐筆核准，核准流程可能遞延，但 2027 年整體供應量沒
有問題 
 公司產能與稼動率狀況？  
 稼動率超過 100% ，供不應求 
 同業宣布兩年內 PD 及 CW Laser 產能擴張 6 倍，公司是否考慮與
產能利用率低的同業結盟以擴充產能？公司 InP 基板戰略庫存可支
應多久？ 
 基板：2027 年沒問題，2028 年正與供應商洽談

 磊晶片多委外生產，公司原有 MOCVD 機台是否封存、出租或出

 公司沒有 MOCVD 機台，不存在出租或轉售問題  
 中系與美系客戶的 200G PD 認證與拉貨比例？公司 200G PD 市占
與優勢？最主要的出貨瓶頸？訂單能見度與 double booking ？ 
 中系與美系都是客戶，不做區分；已有客戶開始準備上量 
 200G PD 仍在上量階段，無法談市占，目前肯定沒有 100G PD
的市占高 
 優勢在良率與效能，規格較同業好，客戶設計模組時可省掉部分
component 等額外成本；公司以 4 吋生產，多數同業仍在 3 吋
甚至 2 吋，價格上不是絕對優勢但具競爭力  
 2027 年基板與磊晶沒問題；中國以外的基板來源 2027 年處於
爬升階段，2028 年以後才會有較有意義的比例

產能本身也不是瓶頸，主要基板廠產能 2026 年翻倍、 2027 年
再翻倍，問題在政治面  
 Double booking 確實存在但規模不明，缺貨讓客戶積極備貨；
公司基本上沒有庫存，生產出來很快就出貨  
 Foundry 訂單能見度到 6M27； PD 方面客戶尚未下 2027 年全年
訂單，但都有給 forecast ，公司已做相應準備  
 近期光通元件大廠與網通晶片大廠的合併案，對光通產業的影響？  
 公司看法正面，收購方過去只有電的晶片，現在補齊光的晶片，
portfolio 相當完整，將與網通晶片龍頭正面競爭  
 可能只是開頭，其他做電的晶片廠商也可能思考類似整合 
 對整體供需不會有太大影響；若競爭使價格有所調整，對產業屬
正面 
 2026 年 100G PD 市占較 2025 年是否有變化？2027 年如何看？  
 2025- 2026 年市占差不多  
 2027 年 100G 可能稍有影響，因 2026 年基板短缺時客戶對供應
商做了調整，但影響不大  
 公司同時開出多個區域的供貨能力，有信心至少維持市占 
 70mW 目前與未來占比？威力富近況？  
 70mW 沒有什麼占比，仍在 sample 階段，同樣依客戶回饋調整
中 
 廠內生產主力為 100mW ，70mW 由合作夥伴提供  
 11/3 董事會通過對威力富的增資案，目的在增加其產能、補齊
缺少的設備，未來威力富將是美國以外很重要的生產基地

 200G PD 所需基板與磊晶片是否與 100G 共用供應鏈？材料漲價能
否轉嫁？ 
 基板相同，磊晶片結構不同，供應鏈可以共用  
 限制出貨的主要是基板，其他沒有問題  
 材料價格會反映給客戶，但有時反映速度跟不上材料價格漲幅  
 200G PD 的競爭者與 100G 世代有何不同？客戶選擇考量？  
 200G 有 SiGe PD 占到一席，但受物理性質限制，模組設計較複
雜，公司視為對自身有利； InP PD 規格仍比 SiGe 好很多 
 客戶選擇公司看的是品質、交期、產能，價格為次要 
 100G 市占相當穩定，200G 剛開始，會積極爭取市占 
 如何看 InP 與 SiGe PD 在光通訊的應用？  
 InP 不會被完全取代，發光必須在這類材料上  
 SiGe PD 大約做到 200G ，再往上有困難，400G PD 仍以 InP 為
主 
 SiGe PD 與 InP PD 規格有差距，模組設計不同，做 SiGe PD 可能
需要更多 know-how；長期兩者共存，並非全面的零和競爭  
 InP 基本上涵蓋所有應用， SiGe 受傳輸距離與電路設計限制，應
用較窄；公司為化合物半導體廠，不會做 SiGe 的規劃 
 為何淨利成長、營業現金流卻下降？  
 主因應收帳款增加尚未收現，應收帳款周轉天數約 45 天，與過
去差異不大，仍在合理的客戶授信期間內  
 新台幣匯率波動對公司的影響？ 
 營運主體在美國子公司，收付款以美元為主，新台幣波動影響不
大 
 公司產能擴充規劃？  
 對威力富增資並擴充產能，作為美國以外的生產基地 
 美國廠將擴充 cleanroom 並補齊瓶頸機台，預計 2Q27 完成 
 2027 年另有計畫，目前不便報告 
 公司稱 2027 年基板供應無虞，但存貨未見相應增加，是否透過長
約確保供應？  
 是，以長約方式確保供應  
 基板非公司直接採購，而是由磊晶廠商向基板廠商鎖量，過程涉
及 prepayment 條件 
 未來費用是否都會維持高檔？ RSA 是否影響每一季？ 
 RSA 依發行日當天市價及既得期間攤銷認列酬勞成本，只要有發
行 RSA 就會依發行辦法認列

台灣 環宇-KY

 費用水準請參考 4Q26 營業費用財測  
 美系 IDM 積極尋找 200G PD 外包產能，是否代表 200G PD 是 1.6T
收發模組的量產瓶頸？200G PD 價格能否維持高檔？  
 據了解該美系 IDM 策略可能有變化；以雷射為主的廠商生產雷
射的營收與利潤遠高於 PD，會自行調整  
 公司不認為 200G PD 是 1.6T 收發模組的瓶頸  
 已有客戶開始上量，公司持續分散基板來源；200G PD 價格相對
100G 肯定較高，但不可能一直維持，量大且供貨穩定後價格自
然會調整 
 GDR 進度？ 
 沒有特別進度，可能會調整資金用途  
 目標在 2026 年底前送件，希望送件前股票不要被列入處置

### Page 1

华为、阿⾥等  20 家单位共同起草发布《智能体发展⾃律公约》。在治理机制⽅⾯，公约提
出积极发展规则表达与内嵌、合规性测试等技术，确保智能体⾏为遵守法律法规与公序良
俗，共同抵制利⽤智能体实施算法压榨、⿊灰产等⾏为。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 4 ⽇， 2026 年世界互联⽹⼤会乌镇峰会 “ 智能体互信互联互操作全球合作创新 —— 携⼿构建
智能互联⽹论坛 ” 正式发布了《智能体发展⾃律公约》（以下简称《⾃律公约》）。《⾃律公约》是
落实《智能体规范应⽤与创新发展实施意⻅》 “ 强化⾏业⾃律 ” 任务部署的具体举措，由中国信息
通信研究院（简称 “ 中国信通院 ” ）、腾讯、蚂蚁、阿⾥、抖⾳等智能体⽣态主体，在中国⽹络社
会组织联合会等组织下共同起草并发布。
《⾃律公约》共提出⼋条⾃律措施，⼀是健全智能体信任体系，⼆是建设智能体互联基础设
施，三是提升智能体互操作⽔平，四是提升智能体安全防护能⼒，五是健全智能体治理机制，
六是保护智能体⽤⼾权益，七是加速智能体赋能千⾏百业，⼋是推动智能体技术普惠共享。

《⾃律公约》的制定发布，是智能体⾏业迈向规范化发展的重要⼀步，将有利于凝聚各⽅共
识，推动⾏业加强⾃律，推动提升智能体互信、互联、互操作⽔平，引导智能体规范应⽤、创
新发展。
下⼀步，各发起单位将带头落实《⾃律公约》约定，主动接受⾏业组织和社会公众监督。同
时，欢迎智能体⽣态中各类主体积极参与公约建设，共同营造开放、可信、安全、普惠的智能
体⽣态。
附：公约全⽂及发起单位名单
智能体发展⾃律公约
智能体是⼈类智慧的延伸，是增进⼈类福祉、促进社会进步和可持续发展的⼯具。为促进智能
体互信、互联、互操作，推动规范应⽤与创新发展，构建开放、可信、安全、普惠的智能体⽣
态，我们共同制定并积极践⾏本公约，⾃觉接受⾏业组织和社会监督。本公约将根据⾏业发展
和政策法规变化定期评估、适时修订，以确保公约的时效性与适⽤性。
第⼀条 健全智能体信任体系。建⽴分层次、可验证的智能体⾝份标识，完善智能体⾝份验证与
管理、权限授予与控制、规则表达与内嵌等机制。降低智能体互信的技术⻔槛，为构建智能体
⽣态建⽴信任锚点。

第⼆条 建设智能体互联基础设施。共建可互联的基础设施，发展智能体注册与解析、能⼒描述
与发现、意图理解与转换、⽹络路由与连接、资源调度与计量、协议兼容与互认等技术与标
准。不断降低智能体跨域发现连接成本，⽀撑智能体互联协同。
第三条 提升智能体互操作⽔平。在合法合规、守牢安全底线前提下，尊重各⽅知识产权与正当
权益，坚持开放协作、平等协商，深化能⼒开放与接⼝适配、任务委托与协同执⾏、分类分级
授权与⾏为边界、权利义务与责任划分等机制。有序降低智能体互操作壁垒，通过多智能体能
⼒整合提升复杂任务交付⽔平。
第四条 提升智能体安全防护能⼒。健全前瞻性⻛险评估、运⾏监测、⾏为管控、异常熔断等技
术体系，努⼒保障模型、插件、技能来源可靠，共建⻛险信息共享与联合处置机制。持续防范
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
⼈⼯智能内⽣缺陷与外部攻击引发智能体越权操作、隐私泄露乃⾄⾏为失控等安全⻛险，打造
安全可控的产品服务。
第五条 健全智能体治理机制。积极发展规则表达与内嵌、合规性测试等技术，确保智能体⾏为
遵守法律法规、公序良俗，共同抵制利⽤智能体实施算法压榨、⿊灰产等⾏为。持续构建健康
向善的发展秩序，提升智能体融⼊经济社会体系的能⼒。
第六条 保护智能体⽤⼾权益。保障⽤⼾知情权、选择权和对重要事项的最终控制权，防范个⼈
信息滥⽤、过度迎合、认知操纵，加强未成年⼈、⽼年⼈等特殊群体保护，培育⽤⼾健康使⽤
素养。不断提升智能体公平性与可及性，保障⽤⼾在智能体时代平等享有发展权。
第七条 加速智能体赋能千⾏百业。坚持服务实体经济，发展低⻔槛智能体开发部署⼯具，合作
研发⾯向真实作业场景、创造实际价值的智能体，推动智能体嵌⼊现有⽣产系统和业务流程，

打造标杆型应⽤。不断降低智能体落地应⽤成本，助⼒⾏业提质增效。
第⼋条 推动智能体技术普惠共享。共促基础共性技术开源开放，参与国际标准研发与规则构
建，通过联合研发、⼈才培训等，打造全球开发者受益的创新⽹络。不断提升技术获取和本地
化部署便利性，让智能体服务惠及更多国家和地区的⽤⼾。

⺠主党在德克萨斯州、佐治亚州、俄亥俄州和爱荷华州取得重⼤胜。虽然媒体的注意⼒⾼
度集中在争夺美国国会控制权的⽃争上，但传统上共和党州的州⻓选举结果可能会在 2028
年总统⼤选之前重塑政治版图。美股期货反弹，纳指期货跌幅收窄⾄ 0.5% 、美元承压、韩
国⾸尔综指转涨。美债逆势⾛强，分裂政府预期正在重塑年末市场格局。
摘要：
⺠主党在美国中期选举中取得压倒性胜利，不仅夺回国会参众两院控制权，更在多个传统共和
党票仓实现历史性突破。全球市场经历了剧烈的 “ 先跌后涨 ” ⾏情：美股期货⼀度跳⽔、纳指期
货跌超 2% ，随后逐步收窄跌幅；美债全线⾛强，⻩⾦重回 4000 美元上⽅，韩股由跌 4% 转为翻
涨。
据新华社援引美国媒体 3 ⽇预测，⺠主党不仅将在中期选举中夺得国会众议院控制权，还连续在
多个关键州击败共和党获得国会参议院席位。据央视新闻，美东时间 11 ⽉ 3 ⽇深夜，特朗普在与
哥伦⽐亚⼴播公司新闻频道的通话中，就⺠主党⼈在中期选举中取得的优势成果向⺠主党⽅⾯
表达了祝贺，称 " 期待与你们合作，为我们国家的利益⽽努⼒ " 。但特朗普同时强调，他拥有完全

的否决权 —— 这意味着即便⺠主党掌控国会，特朗普仍可通过⾏使总统否决权迫使对⽅寻求跨
党派妥协，⽩宫的政策主导权并未因选举结果⽽易⼿。
在⺠主党预计全⾯掌控国会参众两院之后，市场⾏情持续演变。美股股指期货持续反弹，纳指
期货跌幅收窄⾄ 0.48% ，标普 500 指数期货跌幅收窄⾄ 0.4% 。现货⻩⾦重回 4000 美元 / 盎司上
⽅，⽇内涨超 1.7% ；现货⽩银⽇内涨幅扩⼤⾄ 2% ，报 50.85 美元 / 盎司。美债则全线⾛强。亚太
股市⽅⾯，韩国⾸尔综指转涨，此前⼀度跌 4% 。
州级版图剧变，⺠主党构建政策防⽕墙
在州级⾏政权⼒的⻆逐中，⺠主党同样打破了共和党⾃ 2010 年以来的多数垄断地位，多个红州
发⽣历史性逆转。⺠主党⼈ Gina Hinojosa 爆冷击败共和党现任州⻓ Greg Abbott ，成为得州 30 年
来⾸位⺠主党州⻓及⾸位拉美裔州⻓。
在佐治亚州，前亚特兰⼤市⻓ Keisha Lance Bottoms 击败亿万富翁 Rick Jackson ，终结共和党

对该州逾 20 年的控制，并成为美国⾸位当选州⻓的⿊⼈⼥性。在俄亥俄州，⺠主党⼈ Amy Acton
击败共和党⼈ Vivek Ramaswamy ；在艾奥⽡州， Rob Sand 爆冷击败共和党⼈ Zach Lahn 。⺠主
党同时顺利守住宾⼣法尼亚州和纽约州，仅在佛罗⾥达州，共和党⼈ Byron Donalds 险胜。

### Page 2

州级⾏政权⼒的更迭将对未来的选举机制产⽣直接作⽤。各州新任州⻓在任命选举监督官员、
签署选⺠权利法案以及发起针对联邦政策的法律诉讼等⽅⾯拥有⼴泛职权，分析⼈⼠指出，州
级版图的⼤幅重写意味着⺠主党将在选举管理与选⺠权利保护⽅⾯掌握更多主动权，并可能提
前影响 2028 年总统⼤选的基本盘格局。
⺠主党赢得国会控制权，美债⾛强
市场⾛势背后有清晰的逻辑主线。投资者普遍认为，⺠主党若掌控国会，将对特朗普的减税及
亲商政策形成制约，同时对科技和⼈⼯智能⾏业带来更严格的监管压⼒，尤其是数据中⼼建设
相关政策⾯临不确定性。 AI 概念股此前数周已因数据中⼼⽀出增速放缓的担忧⽽有所回调。
⽽⺠主党赢得国会参众两院控制权，意味着特朗普政府将⾯临分权制衡局⾯，市场预期其扩⼤
⾚字的税收和⽀出计划受阻，推动美国国债全线上涨。
美债收益率曲线全⾯下⾏，五年期国债收益率领跌，⼀度下挫 9 个基点⾄ 4.67% ，随后有所收
窄；基准⼗年期收益率下⾏3个基点⾄ 4.93% 。与此同时，美元指数⾛软 0.3% 。

在国债市场此番上涨背后，是债券投资者⻓期以来对美国财政⾛向的忧虑 —— 当前美国国债规
模约达 40 万亿美元。分权政府的形成有望遏制进⼀步扩张⽀出或减税的⽴法空间，从⽽降低国
债增发压⼒。
Wellington Management 投资组合经理 Brij Khurana 表⽰， " 分权政府意味着财政⽀出将少于过去
⼏年。从历史经验来看，每逢分权政府格局形成，对美债⽽⾔通常是个利好信号。 "
与此同时，市场⽬光也已转向另⼀重要时间节点：美国财政部将于周三公布季度国债发⾏计
划。该计划将明确短期内国债的发⾏规模与期限结构，对收益率曲线⾛势具有直接影响，是市
场近期的核⼼观察窗⼝。
野村证券驻悉尼⾼级利率策略师 Andrew Ticehurst 表⽰： " 早期新闻头条和预测市场均显⽰蓝⾊
浪潮的概率正在上升。这⼀情景普遍被认为对美元偏空、对美债偏多，亚洲盘早盘的市场⾛势
与此判断⼀致。 "
Pepperstone 集团研究主管 Chris Weston 表⽰： " 蓝⾊浪潮本⾝或许并不令⼈意外 —— 真正出

乎市场预料的是胜选幅度之⼤。市场初期反应是⻛险的急剧重新定价，投资者难以在政府政策
敏感板块中确⽴合理估值。 "
蓝⾊浪潮冲击市场，美股期货⼀度跳⽔
随着早期选情结果陆续出炉，⺠主党拿下北卡罗来纳州参议院席位，预测平台 Polymarket 上⺠
主党横扫国会两院的概率飙升⾄ 99% 。
标普 500 指数期货跌超 1% ，抹去此前 0.5% 的涨幅；纳斯达克 100 指数期货跌幅扩⼤超 2% 。美元
指数⽇内跌幅超过 0.5% ， 10 年期美国国债收益率下⾏约 7 个基点⾄ 4.89% 。
亚洲股市承压，⽇韩领跌后有所回稳
亚洲股市在此背景下全⾯承压。 MSCI 亚太指数下跌 0.4% 。⽇经 225 指数期货跌超 2% ，⽇本东证
指数跌 1.1% 。韩国综合股价指数（ Kospi ）由盘中最⾼涨 3.2% 急转直下，跌幅⼀度扩⼤⾄ 4% ，
随后转涨，成为此轮选情冲击中波动最为剧烈的市场之⼀。

### Page 3

部分策略师提⽰，本次选举引发的市场波动在历史上往往持续时间有限。 Edward Jones ⾼级策
略师 Angelo Kourkafas 表⽰，选举结果可能起到消除不确定性的 " 出清 " 作⽤， AI 主题⾛向与地缘
政治动态才是驱动市场⾛向年末的核⼼变量。他同时指出，若出现选举驱动的市场⼤幅波动，
或将为⻓期投资者提供逢低布局的机会。
选情演进：⺠主党逐步锁定两院
据 CNBC 选举分析台此前分析预测，⺠主党在众议院 435 个席位中已⾄少锁定 210 席，并快速逼
近取得控制权所需的 218 席⻔槛。
在参议院⽅⾯，南卡罗来纳州⺠主党籍候选⼈安妮 · 安德鲁斯击败共和党籍候选⼈达琳 · 格雷厄
姆，成功翻转⼀个席位；北卡罗来纳州⺠主党籍前州⻓罗伊 · 库珀击败获特朗普⽀持的共和党候
选⼈迈克尔 · 沃特利。随后， DDHQ 预测得克萨斯州⺠主党候选⼈詹姆斯 · 塔拉⾥科将击败共和党
候选⼈肯 · 帕克斯顿，成为 1988 年以来⾸名在该州赢得联邦参议院席位的⺠主党⼈。⾄此，⺠主
党完成参众两院的全⾯翻盘。

在已出炉的关键选举结果中，⺠主党势头明显超出市场预期。除 Roy Cooper 拿下北卡罗来纳州
外，乔治亚州⺠主党现任参议员 Jon Ossoff 亦预计在连任选举中胜出。 Ossoff 因持续抨击特朗普
利⽤总统职位谋取私利⽽在党内崛起为新星。
在众议院⽅⾯，纽约州⺠主党候选⼈ Lasher 及宾⼣法尼亚州候选⼈ Paige Cognetti 均预计胜选。
预测平台 Kalshi 显⽰⺠主党赢得众议院的概率约为 99% ，赢得参议院的概率约为 100% 。有分析
称，市场信号显⽰，共和党在各个层⾯都遭到碾压。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
